3.39
for this example.

Remark 3.42
.

With the help of a computer program (e.g., SageMath), one can verify Conjecture
3.39
for many examples.

4.
A new take on the combinatorics

In this section, our main goal is to establish Theorem
3.22
. In particular, this leads to a new proof of Theorem
3.22
.
(2)
, originally obtained in
[
13
, Thm.4.13]
and
[
14
]
.
As in Remark
2.7
, we may assume that
(C,0)
is a
generic
irreducible planar curve singularity parameterized by:

x(t)=t^{dn},y(t)=t^{dm}+\lambda t^{dm+1}+\sum_{j\geq 2:\penalty\ dm+j\notin\Gamma}\lambda_{j}t^{dm+j},\quad(n,m)=1,m>n,\penalty\ \penalty\ \lambda\in\mathbf{k}^{\times},\penalty\ \penalty\ \lambda_{j}\in\mathbf{k}.

4.1.
From admissible
(dn,dm)
-invariant subsets to
(dn,dm)
-Dyck paths

Definition 4.1
.

Let
(c_{in+j}=c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}
be any given
d\times n
matrix of nonnegative integers s.t. each row
(c_{i,j})_{0\leq j\leq n-1}
is non-decreasing. We use the
identification

\mathbb{Z}/n\cong\{0,1,\cdots,n-1\}
, if no confusion arises.
For each
0\leq i\leq d-1
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}/n
,
denote

\mathrm{rk}_{i,j}((c_{\bullet,\bullet}))=\mathrm{rk}_{in+j}((c_{\bullet,\bullet})):=\mathrm{rk}(in+j,c_{i,j})=m(in+j)-nc_{i,j}.

Remark 4.2
.

Fix any
0\leq u<v\leq d-1
. Then, for
0\leq j\leq n-2
, we have

\mathrm{rk}_{v,j+1}((c_{\bullet,\bullet}))\leq\mathrm{rk}_{u,j}((c_{\bullet,\bullet}))+m\penalty\ \penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ c_{v,j+1}\geq c_{u,j}+(v-u)m;

For
j=n-1
, we have

\mathrm{rk}_{v,j+1}((c_{\bullet,\bullet}))\leq\mathrm{rk}_{u,j}((c_{\bullet,\bullet}))+m\penalty\ \penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ c_{v,0}\geq c_{u,n-1}+(v-u-1)m.

Now, fix
\forall\Delta\cong(c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{A}dm(dn,dm)
.
Denote

\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,in+j}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}:=\mathrm{rk}(in+j,c_{i,j})=m(in+j)-nc_{i,j},\quad\forall 0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1.

Warning
: Under the identification
\mathbb{Z}/n\cong\{0,1,\cdots,n-1\}
, we have
\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,n}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,0}
,
\forall 0\leq i\leq d-1
, but
\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,in+n}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i+1,0}
,
\forall 0\leq i<d-1
.
For simplicity, we also make the following
identification
:

(4.1)

(i,j)=in+j,\quad\forall 0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1.

In particular, for any
0\leq i<d-1
, we have
in+n=(i+1,0)
!

Now, we make the following
construction
s:

(1)

Denote
\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,in+j}^{[0]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}^{[0]}:=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}
.
By induction, for each
1\leq u\leq d-1
, define the
move index

\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}
and
rank vector

(\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,in+j}^{[u]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}^{[u]})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}
from
(\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,in+j}^{[u-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}^{[u-1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}
as follows:
Define
10
10

10

Let’s emphasize again that
\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,u,n}^{[u-1]}:=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,u,0}^{[u-1]}
.

(4.2)

\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}:=\max\{in+j+1:\penalty\ 0\leq i\leq u-1,0\leq j\leq n-1,\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,u,j+1}^{[u-1]}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}^{[u-1]}+m\}.

Note
:
\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}
depends only on
(\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}^{[u-1]})_{0\leq i\leq u,0\leq j\leq n-1})
.
Then, define
(\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,in+j}^{[u]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}^{[u]})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}
by

(4.3)

\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x=in+j}^{[u]}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x}^{[u-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}^{[u-1]}&0\leq x<\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}\text{ or }(u+1)n\leq x<dn;\\
\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x-n}^{[u-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i-1,j}^{[u-1]}&\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}+n\leq x<(u+1)n;\\
\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,un+j}^{[u-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,u,j}^{[u-1]}&\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}\leq x<\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}+n.\end{array}\right.

Equivalently, say,
\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}=kn+\ell
,
0\leq k\leq u
,
0\leq\ell\leq n-1
, then

\displaystyle(\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,0}^{[u]},\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,1}^{[u]},\cdots,\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,dn-1}^{[u]})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle(\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,0}^{[u-1]},\cdots,\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}-1}^{[u-1]},\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,un+\ell}^{[u-1]},\cdots,\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,un+n-1}^{[u-1]},

\displaystyle\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,un}^{[u-1]},\cdots,\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,un+\ell-1}^{[u-1]},\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}}^{[u-1]},\cdots,\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,un-1}^{[u-1]},\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,(u+1)n}^{[u-1]},\cdots,\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,dn-1}^{[u-1]}).

(2)

For each
0\leq j\leq n-1
and
1\leq u\leq d-1
, define a
permutation

\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,u}
of
\{0,1,\cdots,d-1\}
by

(4.4)

\tilde{\mathbf{i}}_{j,u}:=\min\{0\leq i\leq u:\penalty\ in+j\geq\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}\}\penalty\ \penalty\ (\text{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}permutation index});\quad\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,u}:=(\tilde{\mathbf{i}}_{j,u}\penalty\ \cdots\penalty\ u-1\penalty\ u).

Denote

\tilde{\mathbf{i}}_{n,u}:=\tilde{\mathbf{i}}_{0,u}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{n,u}:=\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{0,u}
.
Then,
observe
that

(4.5)

\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}^{[u]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,u}^{-1}(i),j}^{[u-1]}.

Finally, for each
0\leq j\leq n-1
, define the composition

(4.6)

\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j}:=\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,d-1}\circ\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,d-2}\circ\cdots\circ\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,1}.

(3)

For each
0\leq u\leq d-1
,
0\leq i\leq d-1
, and
0\leq j\leq n-1
, define

(4.7)

c_{i,j}^{[u]}:=c_{\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,1}^{-1}\circ\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,2}^{-1}\circ\cdots\circ\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,u}^{-1}(i),j}-(\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,1}^{-1}\circ\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,2}^{-1}\circ\cdots\circ\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,u}^{-1}(i)-i)m.

In particular,
c_{i,j}^{[u]}=c_{\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,u}^{-1}(i),j}^{[u-1]}-(\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,u}^{-1}(i)-i)m
.
Then,
observe
that

(4.8)

\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}^{[u]}=\mathrm{rk}_{i,j}((c_{\bullet,\bullet}^{[u]}))=m(in+j)-nc_{i,j}^{[u]}.

Finally,
define

(4.9)

\Phi_{d}((c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}):=(c_{i,j}^{[d-1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}.

(4)

For each box
(x,y)
(top-left vertex) such that
0\leq x\leq dn-1
,
we may write
x=in+j
,
0\leq i\leq d-1
,
0\leq j\leq n-1
. Then for each
0\leq u\leq d-1
, define

(4.10)

\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{\Delta}^{[u]}(x,y):=d\cdot\mathrm{rk}(x,y)+\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,1}^{-1}\circ\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,2}^{-1}\circ\cdots\circ\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,u}^{-1}(i).

In particular,

(4.11)

\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{\Delta}(x,y):=\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{\Delta}^{[0]}(x,y)=d\cdot\mathrm{rk}(x,y)+i.

Clearly,
\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{\Delta}^{[u]}(x,y+1)=\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{\Delta}^{[u]}(x,y)-dn
.

Proposition 4.3
.

We have:

(a)

\forall\Delta\cong(c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{A}dm(dn,dm)
,
the associated
\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}
,
1\leq u\leq d-1
, are well-defined, and
0<\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{1}<\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{2}<\cdots<\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}\leq(d-1)n
.

(b)

The map

\Phi_{d}:\mathcal{A}dm(dn,dm)\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}(dn,dm):(c_{i,j})\mapsto(y_{i,j}):=\Phi_{d}((c_{i,j})),

is well-defined.

Proof.

We prove by induction on
d
. The case
d=1
is trivial. Suppose that the result holds for ‘
<d
’,
d\geq 2
. For the induction, consider the case ‘
d
’.
Fix any
\Delta\cong(c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{A}dm(dn,dm)
. By inductive hypothesis, we already have:

\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}
,
1\leq u<d-1
, are well-defined, and
0<\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{1}<\cdots<\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-2}\leq(d-2)n
;
(\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}^{[u]})
and

(c_{i,j}^{[u]})
,
0\leq u<d-1
, are well-defined;
(c_{i,j}^{[d-2]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{Y}((d-1)n,(d-1)m)
.

Observe that by definition, we have:
\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}^{[u]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}
,
c_{i,j}^{[u]}=c_{i,j}
,
\forall 0\leq u<d-2
,
i\geq u+1
. Moreover,

\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}^{[d-2]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,d-2}^{-1}(i),j}^{[d-3]}
, and
c_{i,j}^{[d-2]}=c_{\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,d-2}^{-1}(i),j}^{[d-3]}-(\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,d-2}^{-1}(i)-i)m
.
Now, as
(c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{A}dm(dn,dm)
, by Lemma
3.14
,
\exists 0\leq j_{0}\leq n-1
such that

\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,d-1,j_{0}+1}^{[d-2]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,d-1,j_{0}+1}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,d-2,j_{0}}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,d-2,j_{0}}^{[d-3]}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j_{0},d-2}(d-2),j_{0}}^{[d-2]}+m.

Thus, by definition,
\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}
is well-defined, and

(d-1)n\geq\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}\geq\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j_{0},d-2}(d-2)\cdot n+j_{0}+1=\tilde{\mathbf{i}}_{j_{0},d-2}\cdot n+j_{0}+1\geq\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-2}+1.

It remains to show that
(c_{i,j}^{[d-1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{Y}(dn,dm)
. By Corollary
3.21
, this is equivalent to:
(a)
.
\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x+1}^{[d-1]}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x}^{[d-1]}+m
,
\forall 0\leq x<dn-1
;
(b)
.
0\leq\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x}^{[d-1]}\leq mx
,
\forall 0\leq x\leq dn-1
.

Indeed, by inductive hypothesis, we already have
(c_{i,j}^{[d-2]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{Y}((d-1)n,(d-1)m)
. So,
\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x+1}^{[d-2]}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x}^{[d-2]}+m
,
\forall 0\leq x<(d-1)n-1
, and
0\leq\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x}^{[d-2]}\leq mx
,
\forall 0\leq x\leq(d-1)n-1
.

Firstly, we prove
(a)
. Say,
\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}=kn+\ell
,
0\leq k\leq d-1
,
0\leq\ell\leq n-1
.

(1)

If
0\leq x<\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}-1
, then by definition, we have

\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x+1}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x+1}^{[d-2]}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x}^{[d-2]}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x}^{[d-1]}+m.

(2)

If
\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}+n\leq x<dn-1
, then by definition, we have

\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x+1}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x+1-n}^{[d-2]}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x-n}^{[d-2]}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x}^{[d-1]}+m.

(3)

If
\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}\leq x=kn+j<kn+n-1
or
(k+1)n\leq x=(k+1)n+j<\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}+n-1
, then by definition,

\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x+1}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,(d-1)n+j+1}^{[d-2]}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,(d-1)n+j}^{[d-2]}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x}^{[d-1]}+m.

(4)

If
x=\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}-1
, say,
x=in+j
,
0\leq i\leq d-2
,
0\leq j\leq n-1
, then by definition of
\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}=in+j+1
, we have

\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x+1}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,d-1,j+1}^{[d-2]}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{i,j}^{[d-2]}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x}^{[d-1]}+m.

(5)

If
x=kn+n-1
, then by definition,

\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x+1}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,k+1,0}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,d-1,0}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,d-1,n-1}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,k,n-1}^{[d-1]}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x}^{[d-1]}+m.

Here, the inequality follows from Lemma
3.14
.
(3)
.

(6)

It remains to consider the case that
x=(k+1)n+\ell-1
. If
\ell=0
, then
x=kn+n-1
, which is just
(5)
.
Thus, it suffices to consider the case that
\ell\geq 1
. Then by the definition of
\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}
, we have

\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x+1}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,k,\ell}^{[d-2]}<\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,d-1,\ell+1}^{[d-2]}-m=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,d-1,\ell+1}-m\leq\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,d-1,\ell}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,d-1,\ell-1}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x}^{[d-1]}+m.

Here, the last two inequalities follow from Lemma
3.14
.
(1),(3)
.

This completes the proof of
(a)
.

Finally, it remains to prove
(b)
. Indeed, by definition, we have
\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j}^{-1}(i),j}\geq 0
. On the other hand, for any
0\leq x\leq dn-1
, by
(a)
, we have
\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x}^{[d-1]}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x-1}^{[d-1]}+m\leq\cdots\leq\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,0}^{[d-1]}+mx
.
It suffices to show that
\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,0}^{[d-1]}=0
.
In fact, as
0<\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}
,
\forall 1\leq u\leq d-1
, it follows by definition that,
\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{0,u}(0)=0
. Then,
\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{0}(0)=0
, and hence
\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,0}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{0}^{-1}(0),0}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,0,0}=0
. Done.
∎

4.2.
From
(dn,dm)
-Dyck paths to admissible
(dn,dm)
-invariant subsets

First of all, fix any
D=(y_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{Y}(dn,dm)
. Recall that,

y_{in+j}:=y_{i,j};\quad\mathrm{rk}_{in+j}=\mathrm{rk}_{i,j}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+j}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j}:=\mathrm{rk}(in+j,y_{i,j})=m(in+j)-ny_{i,j}.

In Section
3.3.3
, we have provided the recipe for defining:

\displaystyle\mathbf{p}_{u},\penalty\ \penalty\ \vec{\mathrm{rk}}^{[d-u]}=(\mathrm{rk}_{x}^{[d-u]})_{0\leq x\leq dn-1},\quad\mathbf{i}_{j,u},\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathbf{s}_{j,u},\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathbf{s}_{j},\penalty\ \penalty\ \forall d-1\geq u\geq 1,0\leq j\leq d-1;

\displaystyle(y_{i,j}^{[u]})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1},\quad\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{D}^{[u]},\penalty\ \penalty\ \forall 0\geq u\geq d-1.

Now, we make an additional
construction
extending
\vec{\mathrm{rk}}^{[d-u]}
:

(1’)

Denote
\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+j}^{[0]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j}^{[0]}:=\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+j}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j}
.
By induction, for each
d-1\leq u\leq 1
,
define

(\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+j}^{[d-u]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j}^{[d-u]})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}
from
(\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+j}^{[d-u-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j}^{[d-u-1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}
as follows: Say,
\mathbf{p}_{u}=kn+\ell
,
0\leq k\leq u
,
0\leq\ell\leq n-1
. Then define

(4.12)

\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[d-u]}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[d-u-1]}&0\leq x<\mathbf{p}_{u}\text{ or }(u+1)n\leq x\leq dn-1;\\
\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+n}^{[d-u-1]}&\mathbf{p}_{u}\leq x\leq un-1;\\
\mathrm{rk}_{D,(k+1)n+j}^{[d-u-1]}&un\leq x=un+j<un+\ell;\\
\mathrm{rk}_{D,kn+j}^{[d-u-1]}&un+\ell\leq x=un+j<(u+1)n.\end{array}\right.

Equivalently,

\displaystyle(\mathrm{rk}_{D,0}^{[d-u]},\mathrm{rk}_{D,1}^{[d-u]},\cdots,\mathrm{rk}_{D,dn-1}^{[d-u]})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle(\mathrm{rk}_{D,0}^{[d-u-1]},\cdots,\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{p}_{u}-1}^{[d-u-1]},\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{p}_{u}+n}^{[d-u-1]},\cdots,\mathrm{rk}_{D,un+n-1}^{[d-u-1]},\mathrm{rk}_{D,(k+1)n}^{[d-u-1]},\cdots,

\displaystyle\mathrm{rk}_{D,(k+1)n+\ell-1}^{[d-u-1]},\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{p}_{u}}^{[d-u-1]},\cdots,\mathrm{rk}_{D,kn+n-1}^{[d-u-1]},\mathrm{rk}_{D,(u+1)n}^{[d-u-1]},\cdots,\mathrm{rk}_{D,dn-1}^{[d-u-1]}).

By definition, we clearly have
\mathrm{rk}_{x}^{[d-u]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[d-u]}
,
\forall 0\leq x\leq un
.

(2’)

By definition of
\mathbf{s}_{j,u}
,
observe
that

(4.13)

\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j}^{[d-u]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{s}_{j,u}(i),j}^{[d-u-1]}.

(3’)

By definition of
(y_{i,j}^{[d-u]})
, we see that
y_{i,j}^{[d-u]}=y_{\mathbf{s}_{j,u}(i),j}^{[d-u-1]}
. It then follows that

(4.14)

\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j}^{[d-u]}=\mathrm{rk}_{i,j}((y_{\bullet,\bullet})^{[d-u]})=\mathrm{rk}(in+j,y_{i,j}^{[d-u]})=m(in+j)-ny_{i,j}^{[d-u]}.

Proposition 4.4
.

We have:

(a)

\forall D\cong(y_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{Y}(dn,dm)
,
the associated
\mathbf{p}_{u}
,
1\leq u\leq d-1
, are well-defined, and
0<\mathbf{p}_{1}<\mathbf{p}_{2}<\cdots<\mathbf{p}_{d-1}\leq(d-1)n
.

(b)

The map

\Psi_{d}:\mathcal{Y}(dn,dm)\rightarrow\mathcal{A}dm(dn,dm):(y_{i,j})\mapsto(c_{i,j}):=\Psi_{d}((y_{i,j})),

is well-defined.

(c)

For each
1\leq u\leq d-1
and
\mathbf{p}_{u}\leq x<un
, we have

\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[d-u-1]}>\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+n}^{[d-u-1]}.

Proof.

We prove by induction on
d
. The case
d=1
is trivial. Suppose that the result holds for ‘
<d
’,
d\geq 2
. For the induction, consider the case ‘
d
’.
Fix any
D\cong(y_{in+j}=y_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{Y}(dn,dm)
. By Corollary
3.21
, we have:
y_{x+1}\leq y_{x}+m
,
\forall 0\leq x<dn-1
;
0\leq y_{x}\leq mx
,
\forall 0\leq x\leq dn-1
.

(1)

First, we show that
\mathbf{p}_{d-1}
is well-defined and
0<\mathbf{p}_{d-1}\leq(d-1)n
.
Recall that

\mathbf{p}_{d-1}:=\max\{0\leq u\leq(d-1)n:\penalty\ \mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[0]}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+n-1}^{[0]}+m\penalty\ \penalty\ (\Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ y_{x+n-1}\leq y_{x}+m)\}.

If we already have
\mathrm{rk}_{D,(d-1)n}^{[0]}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,dn-1}^{[0]}+m
, then
0<\mathbf{p}_{d-1}=(d-1)n\leq(d-1)n
.
Else, there exists a minimal
0\leq x_{0}\leq(d-1)n
such that
\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[0]}\geq\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+n-1}^{[0]}+m+1
,
\forall x_{0}\leq x\leq(d-1)n
.
Fix any
x_{0}\leq x\leq(d-1)n
. Say,
k_{x}\in\mathbb{N}
is maximal such that
x+k(n-1)\leq dn-1
. That is,
k_{x}=\lfloor\frac{dn-1-x}{n-1}\rfloor
.
Then

mx\geq\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[0]}\geq\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+n-1}^{[0]}+m+1\geq\cdots\geq\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+k_{x}(n-1)}^{[0]}+k_{x}(m+1)\geq\lfloor\frac{dn-1-x}{n-1}\rfloor(m+1).

Observe that for
x=d-1
, we have
mx=(d-1)m<\lfloor\frac{dn-1-x}{n-1}\rfloor(m+1)=d(m+1)
. It follows that
x_{0}\geq d
. Thus, by definition,
0<d-1\leq\mathbf{p}_{d-1}=x_{0}-1\leq(d-1)n-1
. Done.

Now,
(\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+j}^{[1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j}^{[1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}
and
(y_{in+j}^{[1]}=y_{i,j}^{[1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}
are defined, and

\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{x}((y_{\bullet,\bullet}^{[1]}))=mx-ny_{x}^{[1]}
. Say,
\mathbf{p}_{d-1}=kn+\ell
,
0\leq k\leq d-1
,
0\leq\ell\leq n-1
.

(2)

We show that
y_{dn-1}^{[1]}\leq y_{(d-1)n}^{[1]}+m
(
\Leftrightarrow

\mathrm{rk}_{D,(d-1)n}^{[1]}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,dn-1}^{[1]}+m
).
Indeed, if
\ell=0
, then

\mathrm{rk}_{D,(d-1)n}^{[1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{p}_{d-1}}^{[0]}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{p}_{d-1}+n-1}^{[0]}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{D,dn-1}^{[1]}+m.

Here, the inequality follows from the definition of
\mathbf{p}_{d-1}
.
If
\ell\geq 1
, then

\mathrm{rk}_{D,(d-1)n}^{[1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,(k+1)n}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,kn+n-1}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{D,dn-1}^{[1]}+m.

(3)

We show that
\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+1}^{[1]}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[1]}+m
,
\forall 0\leq x<(d-1)n-1
or
(d-1)n\leq x<dn-1
.

(3.1)

If
0\leq x<\mathbf{p}_{d-1}-1
, then

\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+1}^{[1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+1}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[1]}+m.

(3.2)

If
\mathbf{p}_{d-1}\leq x<(d-1)n-1
, then

\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+1}^{[1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+1+n}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+n}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[1]}+m.

(3.3)

If
(d-1)n\leq x=(d-1)n+j<(d-1)n+\ell-1
, then

\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+1}^{[1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,(k+1)n+j+1}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,(k+1)n+j}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[1]}+m.

(3.4)

If
x=(d-1)n+\ell-1\geq(d-1)n
, then

\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+1}^{[1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,kn+\ell=\mathbf{p}_{d-1}}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{p}_{d-1}+n-1}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[1]}+m.

Here, the inequality follows from the definition of
\mathbf{p}_{d-1}
.

(3.5)

If
(d-1)n+\ell\leq x=(d-1)n+j<dn-1
, then

\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+1}^{[1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,kn+j+1}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,kn+j}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[1]}+m.

(4)

We show that
0\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[1]}\leq mx
,
\forall 0\leq x\leq dn-1
.
For any
0\leq i\leq d-1
,
0\leq j\leq n-1
, by definition, we have
\mathrm{rk}_{D,x=in+j}^{[1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j}^{[1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{s}_{j,d-1}(i),j}\geq 0
.
It remains to show:
\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[1]}\leq mx
,
\forall 0\leq x\leq dn-1
.

(4.1)

If
0\leq x<(d-1)n
, then by
(3)
, we have

\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[1]}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,x-1}^{[1]}+m\leq\cdots\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,0}^{[1]}+mx=\mathrm{rk}_{D,0}+mx=mx.

(4.2)

If
(d-1)n\leq x=(d-1)n+j<dn
, then

\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{s}_{j,d-1}(d-1),j}\leq m(\mathbf{s}_{j,d-1}(d-1)\cdot n+j)\leq m((d-1)n+j)=mx.

(5)

Observe that, by Corollary
3.21
, we have shown that
(y_{i,j}^{[1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{Y}((d-1)n,(d-1)m)
.
Besides, for any
\mathbf{p}_{d-1}\leq x\leq(d-2)n
, we have

\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+n}>\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+n+n-1}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+n-1}^{[1]}+m.

So by definition of
\mathbf{p}_{d-2}
, we have
\mathbf{p}_{d-2}<\mathbf{p}_{d-1}
.
Now, by the definition of
\mathbf{p}_{u}
,
d-2\geq u\geq 1
, and the inductive hypothesis applied to
(y_{i,j}^{[1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{Y}((d-1)n,(d-1)m)
, we conclude that
\mathbf{p}_{u}
,
d-2\geq u\leq 1
, are well-defined, and
0<\mathbf{p}_{1}<\cdots<\mathbf{p}_{d-2}\leq(d-2)n
.
Altogether,
0<\mathbf{p}_{1}<\cdots<\mathbf{p}_{d-1}\leq(d-1)n
.
In particular,
(y_{i,j}^{[d-1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}
is defined.
Moreover, again by inductive hypothesis applied to
(y_{i,j}^{[1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1}
,
for each
1\leq u<d-1
, i.e.,
1\leq d-u-1\leq d-2
, and
\mathbf{p}_{u}\leq x<un
, we have

\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[d-u-1]}>\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+n}^{[d-u-1]}.

(6)

We show that
\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}>\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+n}
,
\forall\mathbf{p}_{d-1}\leq x<(d-1)n
.
Indeed, by definition of
\mathbf{p}_{d-1}
, as
x+1>\mathbf{p}_{d-1}
,

\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+n}<\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+1}-m\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}.

(7)

It remains to show that
(y_{i,j}^{[d-1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{A}dm(dn,dm)
.
Observe that by definition, we have

y_{d-1,j}^{[d-1]}=y_{d-1,j}^{[1]};\quad(y_{i,j}^{[d-1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1}=\Psi_{d-1}((y_{i,j}^{[1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1}).

So by inductive hypothesis, we already have
(y_{i,j}^{[d-1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{A}dm((d-1)n,(d-1)m)
.
Altogether, by what we have shown above and Corollary
3.16
, it remains to show that
(d-2)
is admissible:

\exists j\in\mathbb{Z}/n\cong\{0,1,\cdots,n-1\}
such that
\mathrm{rk}_{D,d-1,j+1}^{[d-1]}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,d-2,j}^{[d-1]}+m
.

Indeed, recall that for any
0\leq j\leq d-1
,
1\leq u\leq d-1
, we have
\mathbf{i}_{j,u}:=\min\{0\leq i\leq u:\penalty\ in+j\geq\mathbf{p}_{u}\}
, and
\mathbf{s}_{j,u}(u)=\mathbf{i}_{j,u}
. So,

\mathrm{rk}_{D,d-1,j}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,d-1,j}^{[1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{i}_{j,d-1},j};\quad\mathrm{rk}_{D,d-2,j}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,d-2,j}^{[2]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{i}_{j,d-2},j}^{[1]}.

(7.1)

If
\ell\geq 1
,
then
0\leq k\leq d-2
(as
\mathbf{p}_{d-1}=kn+\ell\leq(d-1)n
), and

kn+\ell-1=\mathbf{p}_{d-1}-1\geq\mathbf{p}_{d-2}
. So by definition,
\mathbf{i}_{\ell-1,d-2}\leq k
.
It follows that

\mathrm{rk}_{D,d-1,\ell}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,k,\ell}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,k,\ell-1}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{D,k,\ell-1}^{[1]}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{i}_{\ell-1,d-2},\ell-1}^{[1]}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{D,d-2,\ell-1}^{[d-1]}+m.

Here, the second inequality follows from the last inequality in
(5)
.

(7.2)

If
\ell=0
, then
0\leq k-1\leq d-2
(as
0<\mathbf{p}_{d-1}=kn\leq(d-1)n
), and
(k-1)n+n-1=\mathbf{p}_{d-1}-1\geq\mathbf{p}_{d-2}
.
So by definition,
\mathbf{i}_{n-1,d-2}\leq k-1
.
As above, it follows that

\mathrm{rk}_{D,d-1,\ell}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,k,0}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,k-1,n-1}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{D,k-1,n-1}^{[1]}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{i}_{n-1,d-2},n-1}^{[1]}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{D,d-2,n-1}^{[d-1]}+m.

Altogether, this shows that
d-2
is admissible.

This completes the proof of Proposition
4.4
.
∎

Remark 4.5
.

\forall D\cong(y_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{Y}(dn,dm)
, and
0\leq u\leq d-1
, by the proof of Proposition
4.4
, we have also shown that

(y_{i,j}^{[d-u-1]})_{0\leq i\leq u,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{Y}(un,um);\quad y_{i,j}^{[d-u-1]}=y_{i,j}^{[d-1]},\quad\forall u+1\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1,\\

In particular,
y_{i,0}^{[d-u-1]}\leq y_{i,1}^{[d-u-1]}\leq\cdots\leq y_{i,n-1}^{[d-u-1]}\leq y_{i,0}^{[d-u-1]}+m,\quad\forall u+1\leq i\leq d-1
.

4.3.
The bijection

Denote
[1,k]:=\{1,2,\cdots,k\}
,
\forall k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.
So far, we have obtained well-defined maps

(\Phi_{d},\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{1},\cdots,\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}):\mathcal{A}dm(dn,dm)\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}(dn,dm)\times\prod_{u=1}^{d-1}[1,un],\quad 0<\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{1}<\cdots<\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1},

and

(\Psi_{d},\mathbf{p}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{p}_{d-1}):\mathcal{Y}(dn,dm)\rightarrow\mathcal{A}dm(dn,dm)\times\prod_{u=1}^{d-1}[1,un],\quad 0<\mathbf{p}_{1}<\cdots<\mathbf{p}_{d-1},

Proposition 4.6
.

We have
\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}=\mathbf{p}_{u}\circ\Phi_{d}
and
\mathbf{p}_{u}=\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}\circ\Psi_{d}
,
\forall 0\leq u\leq d-1
.
Moreover,
\Phi_{d},\Psi_{d}
are inverse to each other. In particular, for any
D\in\mathcal{Y}(dn,dm)
with
\Delta=\Psi_{d}(D)\in\mathcal{A}dm(dn,dm)
, we have

\mathbf{i}_{j,u}=\tilde{\mathbf{i}}_{j,u},\quad\mathbf{s}_{j,u}=\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,u},\quad\mathbf{s}_{j}=\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j},\quad\forall 0\leq j\leq n-1,1\leq u\leq d-1.

Proof.

We prove by induction on
d
. The case
d=1
is trivial. Suppose that the result holds for ‘
<d
’,
d\geq 2
. For the induction, consider the case ‘
d
’.

Step

1
.
We show that
\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}=\mathbf{p}_{u}\circ\Phi_{d}
and
\Psi_{d}\circ\Phi_{d}=\mathrm{id}
.

Fix
\forall\Delta\cong(c_{in+j}=c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{A}dm(dn,dm)
. Say,
D\cong(y_{in+j}=y_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}:=\Phi_{d}((c_{\bullet,\bullet}))\in\mathcal{Y}(dn,dm)
. In other words,
\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+j}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j}:=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,in+j}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}^{[d-1]}
.
For simplicity, denote

\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}:=\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}((c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}),\quad\mathbf{p}_{u}:=\mathbf{p}_{u}((y_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}),\quad\forall 1\leq u\leq d-1.

By definition,
(c_{i,j}^{[d-2]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1}=\Phi_{d-1}((c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1})\in\mathcal{Y}((d-1)n,(d-1)m)
,
c_{d-1,j}^{[d-2]}=c_{d-1,j}
. So,

\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}=\max\{in+j+1:\penalty\ 0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1,\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,d-1,j+1}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}^{[d-2]}+m\}.

Moreover, by inductive hypothesis, we have

\mathbf{p}_{d-2}((c_{i,j}^{[d-2]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1})=\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-2}<\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}.

(1)

We show that
\mathbf{p}_{d-1}=\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}
.

(1.1)

If
\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}<x=in+j+1\leq\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}+n-1
, with
0\leq j\leq n-1
, then by definition, we have

\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,d-1,j+1}>\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}^{[d-2]}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i+1,j}^{[d-1]}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+n-1}+m.

Here, the inequality follows from the definition of
\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}
.

(1.2)

If
\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-2}+n<\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}+n\leq x=in+j\leq(d-1)n
, with
0\leq j\leq n-1
, then
x-n>\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-2}=\mathbf{p}_{d-2}((c_{i,j}^{[d-2]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1})
. It follows that

\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x-n}^{[d-2]}>\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x-1}^{[d-2]}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x+n-1}^{[d-1]}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+n-1}+m.

(1.3)

Notice that by Lemma
3.14
.
(1)
, we have
\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,d-1,j+1}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,d-1,j}+m
,
\forall j\in\mathbb{Z}/n\cong\{0,1,\cdots,n-1\}
.
Now, for
x=\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}=kn+\ell
, with
0\leq\ell\leq n-1
, we have

\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,x}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,d-1,\ell}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,d-1,\ell-1}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,kn+\ell+n-1}^{[d-1]}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+n-1}+m.

Altogether, by definition, this shows that
\mathbf{p}_{d-1}=\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}
, as desired.

(2)

Now, by definition, we have
\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,d-1}=\mathbf{s}_{j,d-1}
,
\forall 0\leq j\leq n-1
. Then for any
0\leq i\leq d-1
,
0\leq j\leq n-1
, it follows that

\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j}^{[1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{s}_{j,d-1}(i),j}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j,d-1}(i),j}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}^{[d-2]},\penalty\ \penalty\ \Rightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ y_{i,j}^{[1]}=c_{i,j}^{[d-2]}.

Then by definition, we have

y_{d-1,j}^{[d-1]}=y_{d-1,j}^{[1]}=c_{d-1,j}^{[d-2]}=c_{d-1,j},\quad\forall 0\leq j\leq n-1.

Moreover, by definition and inductive hypothesis, we have

\displaystyle(y_{i,j}^{[d-1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Psi_{d-2}((y_{i,j}^{[1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1})=\Psi_{d-2}((c_{i,j}^{[d-2]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Psi_{d-2}\circ\Phi_{d-2}((c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1})=(c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1},

and for each
1\leq u\leq d-2
.

\mathbf{p}_{u}=\mathbf{p}_{u}((y_{i,j}^{[1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1})=\mathbf{p}_{u}\circ\Phi_{d-2}((c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1})=\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}((c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1})=\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}.

Altogether, as
\Psi_{d}((y_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1})=(y_{i,j}^{[d-1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}
, this shows that

\Psi_{d}\circ\Phi_{d}((c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1})=(y_{i,j}^{[d-1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}=(c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}.

This completes
Step

1
.

Step

2
.
We show that
\mathbf{p}_{u}=\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}\circ\Psi_{d}
and
\Phi_{d}\circ\Psi_{d}=\mathrm{id}
.

Fix
\forall D\cong(y_{in+j}=y_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{Y}(dn,dm)
. Say,
\Delta\cong(c_{in+j}=c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}:=\Psi_{d}((y_{\bullet,\bullet}))\in\mathcal{A}dm(dn,dm)
. In other words,
\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,in+j}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}:=\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+j}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j}^{[d-1]}
.
For simplicity, denote

\mathbf{p}_{u}:=\mathbf{p}_{u}((y_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}),\quad\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}:=\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}((c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}),\quad\forall 1\leq u\leq d-1.

By definition,
c_{d-1,j}=y_{d-1,j}^{[1]}
,

(y_{i,j}^{[1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{Y}((d-1)n,(d-1)m)
,
(c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1}=\Psi_{d-2}((y_{i,j}^{[1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1})
.
So, by inductive hypothesis, we have

\displaystyle(c_{i,j}^{[d-2]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi_{d-2}((c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi_{d-2}\circ\Psi_{d-2}((y_{i,j}^{[1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1})=(y_{i,j}^{[1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1},

and for each
1\leq u\leq d-2
,

\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}=\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}((c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1})=\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{u}\circ\Psi_{d-2}((y_{i,j}^{[1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1})=\mathbf{p}_{u}((y_{i,j}^{[1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-2,0\leq j\leq n-1})=\mathbf{p}_{u}.

(1)

We show that
\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}=\mathbf{p}_{d-1}
.

(1.1)

Fix any
\mathbf{p}_{d-1}<x=in+j+1\leq(d-1)n
, with
0\leq i\leq d-2
,
0\leq j\leq n-1
.
Define
0\leq\underline{j+1}\leq n-1
by
j+1\equiv\underline{j+1}\mod n
. Then by definition of
\mathbf{i}_{\underline{j+1},d-1}
, we have

x=in+j+1\geq\mathbf{i}_{\underline{j+1},d-1}\cdot n+\underline{j+1}
.
It follows that

\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,d-1,j+1}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,d-1,j+1}^{[1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{i}_{\underline{j+1},d-1}+\underline{j+1}}\geq\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}>\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+n-1}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{D,x-1}^{[1]}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}^{[d-2]}+m.

Here, the first inequality follows from Proposition
4.4
.
(c)
.

(1.2)

Now, for
x=in+j+1=\mathbf{p}_{d-1}
, with
0\leq i\leq d-2
,
0\leq j\leq n-1
, we have

\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,d-1,j+1}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,d-1,j+1}^{[1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,x}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,x-1}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j}^{[1]}+m=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}^{[d-2]}+m.

Altogether, by definition, this shows that
\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{d-1}=\mathbf{p}_{d-1}
, as desired.

(2)

Now, by definition, we have
\mathbf{s}_{j}=\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j}
,
\forall 0\leq j\leq n-1
. Then for any
0\leq i\leq d-1
,
0\leq j\leq n-1
, it follows that

\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,\tilde{\mathbf{s}}_{j}^{-1}(i),j}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{s}_{j}^{-1}(i),j}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j},\penalty\ \penalty\ \Rightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ c_{i,j}^{[d-1]}=y_{i,j}.

In other words, we have

\Phi_{d}\circ\Psi_{d}((y_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1})=(c_{i,j}^{[d-1]})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}=(y_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}.

This completes the proof of Proposition
4.6
.
∎

Corollary 4.7
.

Theorem
3.22
.
(3)
holds:
\forall D\cong(y_{in+j}=y_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{Y}(dn,dm)
, the map
\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{D}
defines a bijection

\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{D}:\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{dn,dm}^{+}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathbb{N},

such that
\lfloor\frac{\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{D}}{d}\rfloor=\mathrm{rk}
, and
\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{D}(\mathcal{R}_{dn,dm}^{+}\setminus D)=\mathbb{N}\setminus\Delta
, with
\Delta:=\Psi_{d}(D)\in\mathcal{A}dm(dn,dm)
.

Proof.

As before, we
identify
any box with its top-left vertex.
Say,
\Delta\cong(c_{in+j}=c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}
. So,
\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{s}_{j}(i),j}
.
Recall that

\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{D}(in+j,y):=d\cdot\mathrm{rk}(x,y)+\mathbf{s}_{j}^{-1}(i)=d(m(in+j)-ny)+\mathbf{s}_{j}^{-1}(i),\quad\forall 0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1.

So,
\lfloor\frac{\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{D}}{d}\rfloor=\mathrm{rk}
is clear.

Next, observe that

\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{D}(in+j,y)\equiv dmj+\mathbf{s}_{j}^{-1}(i)\equiv a_{\mathbf{s}_{j}^{-1}(i),j}\mod dn.

It follows that, for each fixed
0\leq i\leq d-1
,
0\leq j\leq n-1
, we have a bijection

\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{D}:\{(in+j,y):\penalty\ y\in\mathbb{Z}\}\xrightarrow{\simeq}a_{\mathbf{s}_{j}^{-1}(i),j}+dn\mathbb{Z}.

As
\{a_{\mathbf{s}_{j}^{-1}(i),j}:\penalty\ 0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1\}
form a complete congruence class modulo
dn
,
we conclude that

\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{D}:\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}:\penalty\ 0\leq x\leq dn-1\}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
is a bijection.
Moreover,

\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{D}(in+j,y)\geq 0\penalty\ \penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ y\leq\lfloor\frac{m(in+j)}{n}\rfloor=a_{i,j}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ (in+j,y)\in\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{dn,dm}^{+}.

Thus,
\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{D}
restricts to a bijection
\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{D}:\overline{\mathcal{R}}_{dn,dm}^{+}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathbb{N}
.

It remains to show that, for any
x=in+j
with
0\leq i\leq d-1
,
0\leq j\leq n-1
, and
y\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{D}(x=in+j,y)\in\Delta\penalty\ \penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ (x,y)\in\overline{D},\text{ i.e., }y\leq y_{x}=y_{i,j}.

Indeed, recall that the set of
dn
-generators of
\Delta
is given by

A(\Delta)=\{\hat{b}_{k,\ell}=\hat{a}_{k,\ell}-dnc_{k,\ell}:\penalty\ 0\leq k\leq d-1,0\leq\ell\leq n-1\}.

Observe that

\hat{b}_{k,\ell}=\hat{a}_{k,\ell}-dnc_{k,\ell}=d(m(kn+\ell)-nc_{k,\ell})+k=d\cdot\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,k,\ell}+k=d\cdot\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k),\ell}+k.

It follows that

\displaystyle\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{D}(x,y)\in\Delta\penalty\ \penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{D}(x,y)=d\cdot\mathrm{rk}(x,y)+\mathbf{s}_{j}^{-1}(i)\geq\hat{b}_{\mathbf{s}_{j}^{-1}(i),j}=d\cdot\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j}+\mathbf{s}_{j}^{-1}(i)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\mathrm{rk}(x,y)=mx-ny\geq\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j}=mx-ny_{x}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ y\leq y_{x}.

Done.
∎

4.4.
The statistics

Definition 4.8
.

Fix any
\Delta\cong(c_{i,j})\in\mathcal{A}dm(dn,dm)
.

(1)

We say that
b\in\Delta
is called an
(dm)
-cogenerator
if
b\notin\Delta
but
b+dm\in\Delta
.

(2)

Define

(4.15)

\mathcal{GC}((c_{i,j}))=\mathcal{GC}(\Delta):=\{(a,b)\penalty\ |\penalty\ b>a,a\in A(\Delta),\text{ $b$ is an $(dm)$-cogenerator of $\Delta$}\}.

Moreover, for any
(nd)
-generator
a
of
\Delta
, define

(4.16)

\mathcal{GC}(a;(c_{i,j}))=\mathcal{GC}(a;\Delta):=\{(a,b)\in\mathcal{GC}(\Delta)\}.

Lemma 4.9

(
[
13
, Lem.4.1]
)
.

For any
a\in A(\Delta)
, We have

|\mathrm{Gaps}(a)|-|\mathrm{Gaps}(a+dm)|=|\mathcal{GC}(a;\Delta)|.

In particular,

\dim(\Delta)

=

|\mathcal{GC}(\Delta)|
.

Let’s reinterpret the lemma in our terminology.
Fix any
\Delta\cong(c_{i,j})\in\mathcal{A}dm(dn,dm)
as above.
Recall that

\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{\Delta}(x=in+j,y):=d\cdot\mathrm{rk}(x,y)+i=d(mx-ny)+i,\quad 0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1.

Moreover,
\Delta=\sqcup_{a\in A(\Delta)}a+dn\mathbb{N}
, where

A(\Delta)=\{\hat{a}_{i,j}-dnc_{i,j}:\penalty\ 0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1\}
. That is,

(4.17)

\hat{a}_{i,j}-dnc_{i,j}=d(m(in+j)-nc_{i,j})+i=d\cdot\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}+i=\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{\Delta}(in+j,c_{i,j})=:\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{\Delta,i,j}.

Recall that
c_{i,n}:=c_{i,0}+m
. This is compatible with
\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{\Delta,i,n}=\hat{a}_{i,n}-dnc_{i,n}=\hat{a}_{i,0}-dnc_{i,0}=\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{\Delta,i,0}
.
Finally, for any
z_{i,j}\in\mathbb{Z}
,
denote

(4.18)

\hat{z}_{i,j}:=\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{\Delta}(in+j,z_{i,j})=d(m(in+j)-nz_{i,j})+i=\hat{a}_{i,j}-dnz_{i,j},\quad 0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1.

Lemma 4.10
.

\forall z_{i,j}\in\mathbb{Z}
,
0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1
, we have:

\displaystyle\hat{z}_{i,j}\text{ is an $(dm)$-cogenerator of $\Delta$ }

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{\Delta,i,j}>\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{\Delta}(in+j,z_{i,j})\geq\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{\Delta,i,j+1}-dm

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}>\mathrm{rk}(in+j,z_{i,j})\geq\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j+1}-m

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle c_{i,j}<z_{i,j}\leq c_{i,j+1}.

Proof.

Observe that
\hat{z}_{i,j}\equiv\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{\Delta,i,j}(\in A(\Delta))\mod dn
, and
\hat{z}_{i,j}+dm\equiv\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{\Delta,i,j+1}(\in A(\Delta))\mod dn
.
Then the result follows immediately from the definition of an
(dm)
-cogenerator.
∎

Definition 4.11
.

Fix any
\Delta\cong(c_{in+j}=c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{A}dm(dn,dm)
. Recall that
c_{i,n}:=c_{i,0}+m
.

(1)

\forall(p,q)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
,
0\leq p=kn+\ell\leq dn-1
, with
0\leq\ell\leq n-1
, define

(4.19)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\Delta;(p,q))

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\#\{(x=in+j,y)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}:\penalty\ 0\leq x\leq dn-1,\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{\Delta}(x,y)>\hat{\mathrm{rk}}_{\Delta}(p,q),

\displaystyle\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j+1}-m\leq\mathrm{rk}(x,y)<\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}\}.

Here, by
x=in+j
, we mean
0\leq j\leq n-1
.
Equivalently, by Lemma
4.10
and a direct check,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\Delta;(p,q))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\#\{(x=in+j,y)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\penalty\ |\penalty\ 0\leq x<dn,\mathrm{rk}(x,y)>\mathrm{rk}(p,q),c_{i,j}<y\leq c_{i,j+1},

\displaystyle\text{ or: }k<i\leq d-1,\mathrm{rk}(x,y)=\mathrm{rk}(p,q),c_{i,j}<y\leq c_{i,j+1}\}.

(2)

Define
\mathcal{G}(\Delta)=(\mathcal{G}_{0}(\Delta),\cdots,\mathcal{G}_{dn-1}(\Delta))
as the reordering of
\mathcal{G}(\Delta;(p,c_{p}))
,
0\leq p\leq dn-1
s.t.
\mathcal{G}_{0}(\Delta)\leq\cdots\leq\mathcal{G}_{dn-1}(\Delta)
.

Note
: By Lemma
4.9
and Lemma
4.10
,
\forall\Delta\cong(c_{i,j})\in\mathcal{A}dm(dn,dm)
, we have

(4.20)

\dim\Delta=|\mathcal{GC}(\Delta)|=|\mathcal{G}(\Delta)|:=\sum_{x=0}^{dn-1}\mathcal{G}_{x}(\Delta).

Definition 4.12
.

Fix any matrix
(y_{in+j}=y_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}
of nonnegative integers such that
y_{0}=0
.

Denote

y_{dn}=y_{d,0}:=dm
.
Fix any
(p,q)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, with
0\leq p\leq dn-1
.

(1)

For each
0\leq x\leq dn-1
,
define

(4.21)

\displaystyle\epsilon((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q);x)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\#\{y\in\mathbb{Z}\penalty\ |\penalty\ 0<y\leq y_{x+1},\mathrm{rk}(x,y)>\mathrm{rk}(p,q)\}

\displaystyle-\#\{y\in\mathbb{Z}\penalty\ |\penalty\ 0<y\leq y_{x},\mathrm{rk}(x,y)>\mathrm{rk}(p,q)\};

(4.22)

\displaystyle\eta((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q);x)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\delta_{[p,dn)}(x)\cdot[\#\{y\in\mathbb{Z}\penalty\ |\penalty\ 0<y\leq y_{x+1},\mathrm{rk}(x,y)=\mathrm{rk}(p,q)\}

\displaystyle-\#\{y\in\mathbb{Z}\penalty\ |\penalty\ 0<y\leq y_{x},\mathrm{rk}(x,y)=\mathrm{rk}(p,q)\}].

Here,
\delta_{[p,dn)}(x)
is defined as
1
if
p\leq x<dn
, and
0
else.

(2)

For each
0\leq j\leq n-1
,
define

(4.23)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{j}((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q)):=\sum_{i=0}^{d-1}\epsilon((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q);in+j);\quad\epsilon((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q)):=\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\epsilon_{j}((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q));

(4.24)

\displaystyle\eta_{j}((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q)):=\sum_{i=0}^{d-1}\eta((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q);in+j);\quad\eta((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q)):=\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\eta_{j}((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q)).

Note
: By definition, we have
\mathrm{rk}(p,q)=\mathrm{rk}(p^{\prime},q^{\prime})

\Rightarrow

\epsilon_{j}((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q))=\epsilon_{j}((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p^{\prime},q^{\prime}))
.

(3)

Define

(4.25)

\zeta((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q)):=\epsilon((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q))+\eta((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q)).

(4)

(
sweep map
)

Define

\zeta((y_{\bullet,\bullet}))=(\zeta_{0}((y_{\bullet,\bullet})),\cdots,\zeta_{dn-1}((y_{\bullet,\bullet})))
as the reordering of
\zeta((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,y_{p}))
,
0\leq p\leq dn-1
s.t.
\zeta_{0}((y_{\bullet,\bullet}))\leq\cdots\leq\zeta_{dn-1}((y_{\bullet,\bullet}))
.

Note
: If
D\cong(y_{in+j}=y_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{Y}(dn,dm)
, then

\displaystyle\epsilon(D;(p,q))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\#\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}:\penalty\ 0\leq x\leq dn-1,y_{x}<y\leq y_{x+1},\mathrm{rk}(x,y)>\mathrm{rk}(p,q)\};

\displaystyle\eta(D;(p,q))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\#\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}:\penalty\ p\leq x\leq dn-1,y_{x}<y\leq y_{x+1},\mathrm{rk}(x,y)=\mathrm{rk}(p,q)\}.

Proposition 4.13
.

We have

\zeta=\mathcal{G}\circ\Psi_{d}.

Moreover, fix
\forall D\cong(y_{in+j}=y_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{Y}(dn,dm)
, with
\Delta\cong(c_{in+j}=c_{i,j}):=\Psi_{d}((y_{\bullet,\bullet}))\in\mathcal{A}dm(dn,dm)
. Then, for any fixed
p=kn+\ell
, with
0\leq k\leq d-1
,
0\leq\ell\leq n-1
, we have

(4.26)

\mathcal{G}((c_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,c_{p}))=\zeta((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k)n+\ell,y_{\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k),\ell})).

In particular,

e(\Delta)=|(c_{i,j})|=|D|,\quad\dim\Delta=|\zeta(D)|=\mathrm{co}\text{-}\mathrm{dinv}(D).

Remark 4.14
.

In
[
14
]
, a bijection called
\mathcal{D}^{-1}
was constructed (in a more complicated way) and shown to satisfy the same property:
\zeta=\mathcal{G}\circ\mathcal{D}^{-1}
.
It’s well-known that the sweep map
\zeta:\mathcal{Y}(dn,dm)\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}(dn,dm)
is a bijection
[
32
]
. As a consequence,
\mathcal{D}^{-1}=\Psi_{d}
, i.e.,
\mathcal{D}=\Phi_{d}
.

The proof of the proposition occupies the rest of this subsection.
Before the proof, we make some preparations.
In the discussion below,
y\in\mathbb{Z}
is
implicit
everywhere.

Lemma 4.15
.

In Definition
4.12
, denote
\mathrm{rk}_{x}:=\mathrm{rk}(x,y_{x})=mx-ny_{x}
, and
r:=\mathrm{rk}(p,q)
. In particular,
\mathrm{rk}_{dn}=\mathrm{rk}_{0}=0
.
Say,
p=kn+\ell
,
0\leq k\leq d-1
,
0\leq\ell\leq n-1
. Fix any
0\leq j\leq n-1
.

(1)

We have

\epsilon_{j}((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q))=\sum_{i=0}^{d-1}[\#\{y:\penalty\ r<\mathrm{rk}(x=in+j,y)<\mathrm{rk}_{x}\}-\#\{y:\penalty\ r<\mathrm{rk}(x=in+j,y)<\mathrm{rk}_{x+1}-m\}].

Moreover, if
(\tilde{y}_{\bullet,\bullet})
is another such matrix such that,
\exists
a sequence
s_{b},0\leq b\leq n-1
, of permutations of
\{0,1,\cdots,d-1\}
satisfying
s_{0}(0)=0
and
y_{a,b}=\tilde{y}_{s_{b}(a),b}-(s_{b}(a)-a)m
, then

\epsilon_{j}((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q))=\epsilon_{j}((\tilde{y}_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q)).

(2)

For
j\neq\ell
, we have
\eta_{j}((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q))=0
. Moreover,

\eta((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q))=\eta_{\ell}((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q))=\sum_{i=k}^{d-1}[\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{in+\ell})}(r)-\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{in+\ell+1}-m)}(r)].

Here, for any
-\infty\leq a<b<\infty
, we have
\delta_{(a,b)}(c):=1
if
a<c<b
and
0
otherwise.

Proof.

(1)
.
Fix any
0\leq i\leq d-1
, and
x:=in+j
. Observe that

0<y\leq y_{x}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathrm{rk}_{x}\leq\mathrm{rk}(x,y)<mx;\quad 0<y\leq y_{x+1}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathrm{rk}_{x+1}-m\leq\mathrm{rk}(x,y)<mx.

Thus,

\displaystyle\#\{y:\penalty\ 0<y\leq y_{x},\mathrm{rk}(x,y)>r\}=\#\{y:\penalty\ \mathrm{rk}_{x}\leq\mathrm{rk}(x,y)<mx,\mathrm{rk}(x,y)>r\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\#\{y:\penalty\ r<\mathrm{rk}(x,y)<mx\}-\#\{y:\penalty\ r<\mathrm{rk}(x,y)<\mathrm{rk}_{x}\}.

Similarly,

\#\{y:\penalty\ 0<y\leq y_{x+1},\mathrm{rk}(x,y)>r\}=\#\{y:\penalty\ r<\mathrm{rk}(x,y)<mx\}-\#\{y:\penalty\ r<\mathrm{rk}(x,y)<\mathrm{rk}_{x+1}-m\}.

Now, the first formula follows immediately.

It remains to show the second formula. Indeed, for each fixed
x=in+j
, we have

\mathrm{rk}_{x}=\mathrm{rk}(x,y_{x})=\mathrm{rk}(\tilde{x}=s_{j}(i)n+j,\tilde{y}_{s_{j}(i)n+j})=:\tilde{\mathrm{rk}}_{\tilde{x}};\quad\mathrm{rk}(x,y)=\mathrm{rk}(\tilde{x}=s_{j}(i)n+j,\tilde{y}=y+(s_{j}(i)-i)m).

So,

r<\mathrm{rk}(x=in+j,y)<\mathrm{rk}_{x}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ r<\mathrm{rk}(\tilde{x}=s_{j}(i)n+j,\tilde{y}=y+(s_{j}(i)-i)m)<\tilde{\mathrm{rk}}_{\tilde{x}}.

As a consequence, we obtain that

\sum_{i=0}^{d-1}\#\{y:\penalty\ r<\mathrm{rk}(x=in+j,y)<\mathrm{rk}_{x}\}=\sum_{\tilde{i}=0}^{d-1}\#\{\tilde{y}:\penalty\ r<\mathrm{rk}(\tilde{x}=\tilde{i}n+j,\tilde{y})<\tilde{\mathrm{rk}}_{\tilde{x}}\}.

Similarly, for
0\leq j\leq n-2
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{rk}_{x+1}=\mathrm{rk}(x+1=in+j+1,y_{i,j+1})=\mathrm{rk}(\tilde{x}+1=s_{j+1}(i)n+j+1,\tilde{y}_{s_{j+1}(i),j+1})=\tilde{\mathrm{rk}}_{\tilde{x}+1},

\displaystyle\mathrm{rk}(x=in+j,y)=\mathrm{rk}(\tilde{x}=s_{j+1}(i)n+j,y+(s_{j+1}(i)-i)m).

For
j=n-1
, denote
s_{0}(d):=d
, as
s_{0}(0)=0
,
s_{0}
is a bijection on
\{1,2,\cdots,d\}
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathrm{rk}_{x+1}=\mathrm{rk}(x+1=(i+1)n,y_{i+1,0})=\mathrm{rk}(\tilde{x}+1=s_{0}(i+1)n,\tilde{y}_{s_{0}(i+1),0})=\tilde{\mathrm{rk}}_{\tilde{x}+1},

\displaystyle\mathrm{rk}(x=(i+1)n-1,y)=\mathrm{rk}(\tilde{x}=s_{0}(i+1)n-1,\tilde{y}=y+(s_{0}(i+1)-(i+1))m).

Altogether, we have

\sum_{i=0}^{d-1}\#\{y:\penalty\ r<\mathrm{rk}(x=in+j,y)<\mathrm{rk}_{x+1}-m\}=\sum_{\tilde{i}=0}^{d-1}\#\{\tilde{y}:\penalty\ r<\mathrm{rk}(\tilde{x}=\tilde{i}n+j,\tilde{y})<\tilde{\mathrm{rk}}_{\tilde{x}+1}-m\}.

Now, the second formula is clear.

(2)
. For any
x=in+j
,
0\leq i\leq d-1
, if
\mathrm{rk}(x,y)=r=\mathrm{rk}(p,q)
, then
mj\equiv r\equiv m\ell\mod dn
,
\Rightarrow

j=\ell
.
So by definition,
\eta_{j}((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q))=0
if
j\neq\ell
, hence
\eta((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q))=\eta_{\ell}((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q))
.
It remains to describe
\eta_{\ell}((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q))
.
Now,
\mathrm{rk}(x=in+\ell,y)=\mathrm{rk}(p=kn+\ell,q)=r

\Leftrightarrow

y=q+(i-k)m
.
Besides, for
x=in+\ell
, observe that

0<q+(i-k)m\leq y_{x}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathrm{rk}_{x}\leq r<mx;\quad 0<q+(i-k)m\leq y_{x+1}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathrm{rk}_{x+1}-m\leq r<mx.

Finally,
x=in+\ell\geq p=kn+\ell

\Leftrightarrow

i\geq k
.
Thus,

\eta_{\ell}((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q))=\sum_{i=k}^{d-1}[\delta_{[\mathrm{rk}_{in+\ell+1}-m,m(in+\ell))}(r)-\delta_{[\mathrm{rk}_{in+\ell},m(in+\ell))}(r)]=\sum_{i=k}^{d-1}[\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{in+\ell})}(r)-\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{in+\ell+1}-m)}(r)].

Here, we use
\delta_{[a,b}(r)=\delta_{(-\infty,b)}(r)-\delta_{(-\infty,a)}(r)
,
\forall-\infty<a\leq b<\infty
.
∎

Now, fix
\forall D\cong(y_{in+j}=y_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{Y}(dn,dm)
.
Say,
\Delta\cong(c_{in+j}=c_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{A}dm(dn,dm)
.
Recall that for any
0\leq u\leq d-1
,
0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1
, we have

\displaystyle y_{in+j}^{[u]}=y_{i,j}^{[u]}:=y_{\mathbf{s}_{j,d-1}\circ\cdots\circ\mathbf{s}_{j,d-u}(i),j}-(\mathbf{s}_{j,d-1}\circ\cdots\circ\mathbf{s}_{j,d-u}(i)-i)m;

\displaystyle\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+j}^{[u]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j}^{[u]}:=\mathrm{rk}(in+j,y_{i,j}^{[u]})=\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{s}_{j,d-1}\circ\cdots\mathbf{s}_{j,d-u}(i),j}.

In particular,
c_{i,j}=y_{i,j}^{[d-1]}=y_{\mathbf{s}_{j}(i),j}-(\mathbf{s}_{j}(i)-i)m
.
As before,
denote

y_{dn}^{[u]}=y_{d,0}^{[u]}:=dm
.
For simplicity, using Definition
4.12
,
denote

\displaystyle\epsilon^{[u]}((p,q);x):=\epsilon((y_{\bullet,\bullet}^{[u]});(p,q);x);\quad\eta^{[u]}((p,q);x):=\eta((y_{\bullet,\bullet}^{[u]});(p,q);x);

(4.27)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{j}^{[u]}(p,q):=\epsilon_{j}((y_{\bullet,\bullet}^{[u]});(p,q));\quad\eta_{j}^{[u]}(p,q):=\eta_{j}((y_{\bullet,\bullet}^{[u]});(p,q));

\displaystyle\epsilon^{[u]}(p,q):=\epsilon((y_{\bullet,\bullet}^{[u]});(p,q));\quad\eta^{[u]}(p,q):=\eta((y_{\bullet,\bullet}^{[u]});(p,q));\quad\zeta^{[u]}(p,q):=\zeta((y_{\bullet,\bullet}^{[u]});p,q).

Moreover, for
u=0
, we may simply drop the superscript
[0]
.

Corollary 4.16
.

\forall 0\leq u\leq d-1
,
\forall 0\leq j\leq n-1
, we have

(4.28)

\epsilon_{j}^{[u]}(p,q)=\epsilon_{j}(p,q).

In particular,
\epsilon^{[u]}(p,q)=\epsilon(p,q)
.

Proof.

This follows immediately from Lemma
4.15
.
(1)
.
∎

Lemma 4.17
.

Fix any
0\leq p=kn+\ell\leq dn-1
, with
0\leq k\leq d-1
,
0\leq\ell\leq n-1
. Then,

\forall 1\leq u\leq d-1
,
\forall 0\leq j\leq n-1
, we have

(4.29)

\eta^{[d-u-1]}(kn+\ell,y_{k,\ell}^{[d-u-1]})=\eta^{[d-u]}(\mathbf{s}_{\ell,u}^{-1}(k)n+\ell,y_{\mathbf{s}_{\ell,u}^{-1}(k),\ell}^{[d-u]}).

So,
\eta(kn+\ell,y_{k,\ell})=\eta^{[d-1]}(\mathbf{s}_{\ell}^{-1}(k)n+\ell,y_{\mathbf{s}_{\ell}^{-1}(k),\ell}^{[d-1]})
. Equivalently,

\eta(\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k)n+\ell,y_{\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k),\ell})=\eta^{[d-1]}(p,y_{p}^{[d-1]})
.

Proof.

Recall that

\displaystyle\mathbf{p}_{u}:=\max\{0\leq x\leq un:\penalty\ \mathrm{rk}_{D,x}^{[d-u-1]}\leq\mathrm{rk}_{D,x+n-1}^{[d-u-1]}+m\};

\displaystyle\mathbf{i}_{j,u}:=\min\{0\leq i\leq u:\penalty\ in+j\geq\mathbf{p}_{u}\},\quad\mathbf{s}_{j,u}:=(\mathbf{i}_{j,u}\penalty\ \cdots\penalty\ u-1\penalty\ u),\quad 0\leq j\leq n-1.

In particular,
\mathbf{s}_{j,u}
is identity on
[u+1,d-1]
.
Besides,
\mathbf{i}_{n,u}:=\mathbf{i}_{0,u}
and
\mathbf{s}_{n,u}:=\mathbf{s}_{0,u}
, and

\mathbf{i}_{j,u}+\lfloor\frac{j+1}{n}\rfloor\geq\mathbf{i}_{j+1,u}\geq\mathbf{i}_{j,u}+\lfloor\frac{j+1}{n}\rfloor-1.

Say,
\mathbf{p}_{u}=k_{u}n+\ell_{u}
,
0\leq k_{u}\leq u
,
0\leq\ell_{u}\leq n-1
.
Denote
\tilde{k}:=\mathbf{s}_{\ell}^{-1}(k)
, and

r:=\mathrm{rk}_{D,k,\ell}^{[d-u-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,\tilde{k},\ell}^{[d-u]}\geq 0.

First of all, as
\mathbf{s}_{\bullet,u}
is identity on
[u+1,d-1]
, we have

\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j}^{[d-u-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j}^{[d-u]}
,
\forall u+1\leq i\leq d-1
.
In particular, for any
u+1\leq v\leq d-1
, we have

(4.30)

\sum_{i=v}^{d-1}[\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+\ell}^{[d-u-1]})}(r)-\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+\ell+1}^{[d-u-1]}-m)}(r)]=\sum_{i=v}^{d-1}[\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+\ell}^{[d-u]})}(r)-\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+\ell+1}^{[d-u]}-m)}(r)].

Notice
that if
\ell=n-1
, we further have
\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,un+\ell+1}^{[d-u-1]}-m)}(r)=\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,un+\ell+1}^{[d-u]}-m)}(r)
.
Thus, if
k\geq u+1
, then
\mathbf{s}_{\ell}^{-1}(k)=k
, and by Lemma
4.15
.
(2)
, this already shows the result.

From now on
, we assume that
0\leq k\leq u
, hence so is
\tilde{k}=\mathbf{s}_{\ell}^{-1}(k)
.
Again by Lemma
4.15
.
(2)
, it remains to show that

(4.31)

\sum_{i=k}^{u}[\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+\ell}^{[d-u-1]})}(r)-\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+\ell+1}^{[d-u-1]}-m)}(r)]=\sum_{i=\tilde{k}}^{u}[\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+\ell}^{[d-u]})}(r)-\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+\ell+1}^{[d-u]}-m)}(r)].

For simplicity,
denote
the left hand side by
\mathrm{LHS}_{\ell}
and the right hand side by
\mathrm{RHS}_{\ell}
.

(1)

If
p=kn+\ell<\mathbf{p}_{u}
, then by definition,
\mathbf{i}_{\ell,u}\geq k+1
, hence
\mathbf{i}_{\ell+1,u}\geq k
.
It follows that
\mathbf{s}_{\ell,u}
and
\mathbf{s}_{\ell+1,u}
restrict to bijections on
[k,u]:=\{k,k+1,\cdots,u\}
, and
\mathbf{s}_{\ell,u}(k)=k=\mathbf{s}_{\ell,u}^{-1}(k)=\tilde{k}
.

•

If
0\leq\ell\leq n-2
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{LHS}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k}^{u}\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{s}_{\ell,u}^{-1}(i),\ell}^{[d-u]})}(r)-\sum_{i=k}^{u}\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{s}_{\ell+1,u}^{-1}(i),\ell+1}^{[d-u]}-m)}(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\tilde{i}=k}^{u}\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,\tilde{i},\ell}^{[d-u]})}(r)-\sum_{\tilde{i}=k}^{u}\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,\tilde{i},\ell+1}^{[d-u]}-m)}(r)=\mathrm{RHS}_{\ell},

as desired.

•

If
\ell=n-1
, then
\mathbf{i}_{\ell+1,u}\geq\mathbf{i}_{\ell,u}\geq k+1
. So,
\mathbf{s}_{0,u}(k)=k=\mathbf{s}_{0,u}^{-1}(k)
. Now,

\displaystyle\mathrm{LHS}_{n-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k}^{u}\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{s}_{n-1,u}^{-1}(i),n-1}^{[d-u]})}(r)-\sum_{i=k}^{u}\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{s}_{0,u}^{-1}(i+1),0}^{[d-u]}-m)}(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\tilde{i}=k}^{u}\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,\tilde{i},\ell}^{[d-u]})}(r)-\sum_{\tilde{i}=k}^{u}\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,\tilde{i}+1,0}^{[d-u]}-m)}(r)=\mathrm{RHS}_{n-1},

as desired.

(2)

It remains to consider the case that
\mathbf{p}_{u}\leq p<(u+1)n
. Then by definition,
\mathbf{i}_{\ell,u}\leq k
.

(2.1)

Suppose that
0\leq\ell\leq n-2
.
Then, for each
k\leq i\leq u
, by Proposition
4.4
.
(c)
and Remark
4.5
(
0\leq\ell\leq n-2
), we have

r=\mathrm{rk}_{D,kn+\ell}^{[d-u-1]}>\mathrm{rk}_{D,(k+1)n+\ell}^{[d-u-1]}>\cdots>\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+\ell}^{[d-u-1]}\geq\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+\ell+1}^{[d-u-1]}-m.

So,
\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+\ell}^{[d-u-1]})}(r)=\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+\ell+1}^{[d-u-1]}-m)}(r)=0
, and hence
\mathrm{LHS}_{\ell}=0
.
As a consequence, it remains to show that
\mathrm{RHS}_{\ell}=0
.

•

If
k=\mathbf{i}_{\ell,u}
, then
\tilde{k}=\mathbf{s}_{\ell,u}^{-1}(k)=u
.
In this case, by Remark
4.5
, we have

r=\mathrm{rk}_{D,u,\ell}^{[d-u]}\geq\mathrm{rk}_{D,u,\ell+1}^{[d-u]}-m
. So,

\mathrm{RHS}_{\ell}=\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,un+\ell}^{[d-u]})}(r)-\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,un+\ell+1}^{[d-u]}-m)}(r)=0.

•

If
\mathbf{i}_{\ell,u}+1\leq k\leq u
, then
\mathbf{i}_{\ell,u}\leq\tilde{k}=\mathbf{s}_{\ell,u}^{-1}(k)=k-1\leq u-1
.
Notice that
\tilde{p}=\tilde{k}n+\ell\geq\mathbf{i}_{\ell,u}n+\ell\geq\mathbf{p}_{u}>\mathbf{p}_{u-1}
. Then for each
\tilde{k}\leq i\leq u-1
, again by Proposition
4.4
.
(c)
and Remark
4.5
(
0\leq\ell\leq n-2
), we have

\sum_{i=\tilde{k}}^{u-1}[\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+\ell}^{[d-u]})}(r)-\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+\ell+1}^{[d-u]}-m)}(r)]=0.

It remains to show that
\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,un+\ell}^{[d-u]})}(r)-\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,un+\ell+1}^{[d-u]}-m)}(r)=0
.
Indeed, by Proposition
4.4
.
(c)
,

\mathrm{rk}_{D,un+\ell}^{[d-u]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{i}_{\ell,u},\ell}^{[d-1-u]}>\mathrm{rk}_{D,k,\ell}^{[d-1-u]}=r,\penalty\ \penalty\ \Rightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,un+\ell}^{[d-u]})}(r)=1.

On the other hand, we have
\mathbf{i}_{\ell+1,u}\leq\mathbf{i}_{\ell,u}\leq k-1
. In particular,
(k-1)n+\ell+1\geq\mathbf{i}_{\ell,u}n+\ell+1\geq\mathbf{p}_{u}+1
. So by definition,
\mathrm{rk}_{D,k-1,\ell+1}^{[d-u-1]}>\mathrm{rk}_{D,k,\ell}^{[d-u-1]}+m=r+m
.
Thus by Proposition
4.4
.
(c)
, we have

\mathrm{rk}_{D,un+\ell+1}^{[d-u]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{i}_{\ell+1,u},\ell+1}^{[d-u-1]}>\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{i}_{\ell+1,u}+1,\ell+1}^{[d-u-1]}>\cdots>\mathrm{rk}_{D,k-1,\ell+1}^{[d-u-1]}>r+m,\penalty\ \penalty\ \Rightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,un+\ell+1}^{[d-u]}-m)}(r)=1.

Altogether, we again have
\mathrm{RHS}_{\ell}=0=\mathrm{LHS}_{\ell}
.

(2.2)

We’re left with the case
\ell=n-1
. So,
\mathbf{i}_{n-1,u}+1\geq\mathbf{i}_{0,u}\geq\mathbf{i}_{n-1,u}
.
Now, for each
k\leq i\leq u-1
, by Proposition
4.4
.
(c)
and Remark
4.5
, we have

r=\mathrm{rk}_{D,kn+n-1}^{[d-u-1]}>\mathrm{rk}_{D,(k+1)n+n-1}^{[d-u-1]}>\cdots>\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+n-1}^{[d-u-1]}\geq\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+n}^{[d-u-1]}-m.

So,
\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+n-1}^{[d-u-1]})}(r)=\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+n}^{[d-u-1]}-m)}(r)=0
.
Moreover, as
\mathbf{i}_{n-1,u}\leq k\leq u
, by Proposition
4.4
.
(c)
, we have

\mathrm{rk}_{D,un+n-1}^{[d-u-1]}>\cdots>\mathrm{rk}_{D,k,n-1}^{[d-u-1]}=r,\penalty\ \penalty\ \Rightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,un+n-1}^{[d-u-1]})}(r)=0.

Altogether,

(4.32)

\mathrm{LHS}_{n-1}=-\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,(u+1)n}^{[d-u-1]}-m)}(r).

Recall that
\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,(u+1)n}^{[d-u-1]}-m)}(r)=\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,(u+1)n}^{[d-u]}-m)}(r)
.

•

If
k=\mathbf{i}_{n-1,u}
, then
\tilde{k}=\mathbf{s}_{n-1,u}^{-1}(k)=u
.
In this case,

\mathrm{RHS}_{n-1}=\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,un+n-1}^{[d-u]})}(r)-\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,(u+1)n}^{[d-u]}-m)}(r).

By above, it remains to show that
\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,un+n-1}^{[d-u]})}(r)=0
.
Indeed,

\mathrm{rk}_{D,un+n-1}^{[d-u]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{i}_{n-1,u},n-1}^{[d-u-1]}=r,\penalty\ \penalty\ \Rightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,un+n-1}^{[d-u]})}(r)=0.

•

If
\mathbf{i}_{n-1,u}+1\leq k\leq u
, then
\mathbf{i}_{n-1,u}\leq\tilde{k}=\mathbf{s}_{n-1,u}^{-1}(k)=k-1\leq u-1
.
Notice that
\tilde{p}=\tilde{k}n+n-1\geq\mathbf{i}_{n-1,u}n+n-1\geq\mathbf{p}_{u}>\mathbf{p}_{u-1}
. Then for each
\tilde{k}\leq i\leq u-2
, again by Proposition
4.4
.
(c)
and Remark
4.5
, we have

\sum_{i=\tilde{k}}^{u-2}[\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,in+n-1}^{[d-u]})}(r)-\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,(i+1)n}^{[d-u]}-m)}(r)]=0.

Second, as
u-1\geq\tilde{k}
, we have
r=\mathrm{rk}_{D,\tilde{k},n-1}^{[d-u]}>\mathrm{rk}_{D,\tilde{k}+1,n-1}^{[d-u]}>\cdots>\mathrm{rk}_{D,u-1,n-1}^{[d-u]}
.
So,
\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,(u-1)n+n-1}^{[d-u]})}(r)=0
.
Third,
\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,un+n-1}^{[d-u]})}(r)=\delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{i}_{n-1,u},n-1}^{[d-u-1]})}(r)
. As
\mathbf{i}_{n-1,u}\leq k-1\leq u-1
, we have

\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{i}_{n-1,u},n-1}^{[d-u-1]}>\cdots>\mathrm{rk}_{D,k-1,n-1}^{[d-u-1]}>\mathrm{rk}_{D,k,n-1}^{[d-u-1]}=r,\penalty\ \penalty\ \Rightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,un+n-1}^{[d-u]})}(r)=1.

Finally, we have
\mathbf{i}_{n-1,u}\leq\mathbf{i}_{0,u}\leq\mathbf{i}_{n-1,u}+1\leq k
. So,
kn\geq(\mathbf{i}_{n-1,u}+1)n>\mathbf{i}_{n-1,u}n+n-1\geq\mathbf{p}_{u}
. Then by definition of
\mathbf{p}_{u}
, we have

\mathrm{rk}_{D,k,0}^{[d-u-1]}>\mathrm{rk}_{D,k,n-1}^{[d-u-1]}+m=r+m
.
On the other hand, as
\mathbf{i}_{0,u}\leq k\leq u
, by Proposition
4.4
.
(c)
, we now have

\mathrm{rk}_{D,un}^{[d-u]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{i}_{0,u},0}^{[d-u-1]}>\cdots>\mathrm{rk}_{D,k,0}^{[d-u-1]}>r+m,\penalty\ \penalty\ \Rightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \delta_{(-\infty,\mathrm{rk}_{D,un}^{[d-u]}-m)}(r)=1.

Altogether, we obtain that

\mathrm{RHS}_{n-1}=-\mathrm{rk}_{D,(u+1)n}^{[d-u]}=-\mathrm{rk}_{D,(u+1)n}^{[d-u-1]}=\mathrm{LHS}_{n-1}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Finally, recall that
c_{in+j}=c_{i,j}=y_{i,j}^{[d-1]}
.
Moreover,
c_{i,j}\leq c_{i,j+1}
,
\forall 0\leq j\leq n-1
, with
c_{i,n}:=c_{i,0}+m
.
As before,
c_{dn}=c_{d,0}:=dm
.
Now, for each
x=in+j
,
0\leq i\leq d-1
,
0\leq j\leq n-1
,
denote

\displaystyle\tilde{\epsilon}((p,q);x):=\#\{y\in(c_{i,j},c_{i,j+1}]:\mathrm{rk}(x,y)>\mathrm{rk}(p,q)\};

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}((p,q);x):=\delta_{[p,dn)}(x)\cdot[\#\{y\in[c_{i,j},c_{i,j+1}):\mathrm{rk}(x,y)=\mathrm{rk}(p,q)\};

(4.33)

\displaystyle\tilde{\epsilon}_{j}(p,q):=\sum_{i=0}^{d-1}\tilde{\epsilon}((p,q);in+j);\quad\tilde{\epsilon}(p,q):=\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\tilde{\epsilon}_{j}(p,q);

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}_{j}(p,q):=\sum_{i=0}^{d-1}\tilde{\eta}((p,q);in+j);\quad\tilde{\eta}(p,q):=\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\tilde{\eta}_{j}(p,q).

Then,

\mathcal{G}((c_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,q))=\tilde{\epsilon}(p,q)+\tilde{\eta}(p,q).

Moreover, observe that

(4.34)

\tilde{\epsilon}((p,q);x)=\epsilon^{[d-1]}((p,q);x),\quad\tilde{\eta}((p,q);x)=\eta^{[d-1]}((p,q);x),\quad\forall 0\leq j\leq n-2.

Lemma 4.18
.

\forall 0\leq j\leq n-1
, we have

(4.35)

\epsilon_{j}^{[d-1]}(p,q)=\tilde{\epsilon}_{j}(p,q).

In particular,
\epsilon^{[d-1]}(p,q)=\tilde{\epsilon}(p,q)
.

Proof.

This is trivial if
j\leq n-2
. It remains to consider the case
j=n-1
.
Fix
\mathbf{c}\geq(d-1)m
, then

\displaystyle\tilde{\epsilon}_{n-1}(p,q)-\epsilon_{n-1}^{[d-1]}(p,q)=\sum_{i=0}^{d-1}(\tilde{\epsilon}((p,q);in+n-1)-\epsilon^{[d-1]}((p,q);in+n-1))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{d-1}(\#\{y\in(0,c_{i,n}]:\mathrm{rk}(in+n-1,y)>\mathrm{rk}(p,q)\}-\#\{y\in(0,c_{i+1,0}]:\mathrm{rk}(in+n-1,y)>\mathrm{rk}(p,q)\})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{d-1}\#\{\tilde{y}\in(-im,c_{i,0}-(i-1)m]:\mathrm{rk}(n-1,\tilde{y})>\mathrm{rk}(p,q)\}

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=0}^{d-1}\#\{\tilde{y}\in(-im,c_{i+1,0}-im]:\mathrm{rk}(n-1,\tilde{y})>\mathrm{rk}(p,q)\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{d-1}\#\{\tilde{y}\in(-\mathbf{c},c_{i,0}-(i-1)m]:\mathrm{rk}(n-1,\tilde{y})>\mathrm{rk}(p,q)\}

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=0}^{d-1}\#\{\tilde{y}\in(-\mathbf{c},c_{i+1,0}-im]:\mathrm{rk}(n-1,\tilde{y})>\mathrm{rk}(p,q)\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.\quad(c_{d,0}-(d-1)m=m=c_{0,0}-(0-1)m)

∎

Lemma 4.19
.

\forall 0\leq j\leq n-1
, we have

(4.36)

\eta_{j}^{[d-1]}(p,c_{p})=\tilde{\eta}_{j}(p,c_{p}).

In particular,
\eta^{[d-1]}(p,c_{p})=\tilde{\eta}(p,c_{p})
.

Proof.

Say,
p=kn+\ell
,
0\leq k\leq d-1
,
0\leq\ell\leq n-1
.
By a similar argument as in Lemma
4.17
, it’s clear that
\tilde{\eta}_{j}(p,c_{p})=0
if
j\neq\ell
.
Thus,
\tilde{\eta}(p,c_{p})=\tilde{\eta}_{\ell}(p,c_{p})
. It remains to show that

\eta_{\ell}^{[d-1]}(p,c_{p})=\tilde{\eta}_{\ell}(p,c_{p})
.
In fact, the case
\ell\neq n-1
follows immediately from the observation (
4.34
).
Thus, we’re left with the case
\ell=n-1
.

Now, by definition, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}_{n-1}((p,q))-\eta^{[d-1]}_{n-1}((p,q))=\sum_{i=0}^{d-1}(\tilde{\eta}((p,q);in+n-1)-\eta^{[d-1]}((p,q));in+n-1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k}^{d-1}(\#\{y\in(0,c_{i,n}]:\mathrm{rk}(in+n-1,y)=\mathrm{rk}(p,q)\}-\#\{y\in(0,c_{i+1,0}]:\mathrm{rk}(in+n-1,y)=\mathrm{rk}(p,q)\}).

From now on, take
q=c_{p}=c_{k,n-1}
.
Fix
\mathbf{c}\geq(d-1)m
.
Then,

\displaystyle\tilde{\eta}((p,q))-\eta^{[d-1]}((p,q))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=k}^{d-1}(\#\{\tilde{y}\in(-\mathbf{c},c_{i,n}-im]:\mathrm{rk}(\ell,\tilde{y})=\mathrm{rk}(p,q)\}-\#\{\tilde{y}\in(-\mathbf{c},c_{i+1,0}-im]:\mathrm{rk}(\ell,\tilde{y})=\mathrm{rk}(p,q)\})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\#\{\tilde{y}\in(c_{k,0}-(k-1)m,m]:\mathrm{rk}(n-1,\tilde{y})=\mathrm{rk}(p,q)\}.

Here,
c_{i,n}-im=c_{i,0}-(i-1)m
,
c_{d,0}-(d-1)m=m
, and
c_{k.0}\leq a_{k,0}=km
.
Moreover, if
\mathrm{rk}(n-1,\tilde{y})=\mathrm{rk}(p,c_{p})
, then
\tilde{y}=c_{k,n-1}-km\leq c_{k,0}-(k-1)m
.
Thus,
\tilde{\eta}((c_{a,b});(p,c_{p}))-\eta((c_{a,b});(p,c_{p}))=0
, as desired.
∎

Now, it’s clear how to prove Proposition
4.13
.

Proof of Proposition
4.13
.

Fix
\forall D\cong(y_{in+j}=y_{i,j})_{0\leq i\leq d-1,0\leq j\leq n-1}\in\mathcal{Y}(dn,dm)
, with
\Delta\cong(c_{\bullet,\bullet}):=\Psi_{d}((y_{\bullet,\bullet}))\in\mathcal{A}dm(dn,dm)
.
Recall that
c_{i,j}=y_{i,j}^{[d-1]}=y_{\mathbf{s}_{j}(i),j}-(\mathbf{s}_{j}(i)-i)m
, and
\mathrm{rk}_{\Delta,i,j}=\mathrm{rk}(in+j,c_{i,j})=\mathrm{rk}_{D,i,j}^{[d-1]}=\mathrm{rk}_{D,\mathbf{s}_{j}(i),j}=\mathrm{rk}(\mathbf{s}_{j}(i)n+j,y_{\mathbf{s}_{j}(i),j})
. In particular,
e(\Delta)=|(c_{i,j})|=|D|
is clear.

Now, fix any
p=kn+\ell
,
0\leq k\leq d-1
,
0\leq\ell\leq n-1
.
By definition, we have
\epsilon_{j}^{[d-1]}(p,c_{p})=\epsilon_{j}^{[d-1]}(\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k)n+\ell,y_{\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k),\ell})
. Then by Corollary
4.16
, we have
\epsilon_{j}^{[d-1]}(\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k)n+\ell,y_{\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k),\ell})=\epsilon_{j}(\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k)n+\ell,y_{\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k),\ell})
.
Combined with Lemma
4.18
, we then obtain:

(4.37)

\tilde{\epsilon}_{j}(p,c_{p})=\epsilon_{j}^{[d-1]}(p,y_{p}^{[d-1]})=\epsilon_{j}(\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k)n+\ell,y_{\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k),\ell}).

In particular,
\tilde{\epsilon}(p,c_{p})=\epsilon^{[d-1]}(p,y_{p}^{[d-1]})=\epsilon(\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k)n+\ell,y_{\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k),\ell})
.

On the other hand, by Lemma
4.17
, and Lemma
4.19
, we have

\tilde{\eta}_{j}(p,c_{p})=\eta_{j}^{[d-1]}(p,y_{p}^{[d-1]})=\eta_{j}(\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k)n+\ell,y_{\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k),\ell}).

In particular,
\tilde{\eta}(p,c_{p})=\eta^{[d-1]}(p,y_{p}^{[d-1]})=\eta(\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k)n+\ell,y_{\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k),\ell})
.

Altogether, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}((c_{\bullet,\bullet});(p,c_{p}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{\epsilon}((p,c_{p}))+\tilde{\eta}((p,c_{p}))=\epsilon^{[d-1]}((p,y_{p}^{[d-1]}))+\eta^{[d-1]}((p,y_{p}^{[d-1]}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon(\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k)n+\ell,y_{\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k),\ell})+\eta(\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k)n+\ell,y_{\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k),\ell})=\zeta((y_{\bullet,\bullet});(\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k)n+\ell,y_{\mathbf{s}_{\ell}(k),\ell})).

In particular,
\mathcal{G}((c_{\bullet,\bullet}))=\zeta((c_{a,b}))
, and hence
\dim\Delta=|\mathcal{G}(\Delta)|=|\zeta(D)|
.

Finally,
|\zeta(D)|=\mathrm{co}\text{-}\mathrm{dinv}(D)
follows from a standard argument.
For completeness, we include the details.
Observe that for
x=p
, we have
\mathrm{rk}(x,y)\geq\mathrm{rk}(p,y_{p})

\Leftrightarrow

y\leq y_{p}=y_{x}
. So by definition,
|\zeta(D)|=\mathrm{I}+\mathrm{II}
, with

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\#\{(p,x,y):0\leq x<p\leq dn-1,y_{x}<y\leq y_{x+1},\mathrm{rk}(x,y)>\mathrm{rk}(p,y_{p})\};

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\#\{(p,x,y):0\leq p<x\leq dn-1,y_{x}<y\leq y_{x+1},\mathrm{rk}(x,y)\geq\mathrm{rk}(p,y_{p})\}.

In addition,
\forall 0\leq p\leq dn-1
,
0\leq x\leq dn-1
,
y_{x}<y\leq y_{x+1}
, observe that the pair
(p,y_{p})
,
(x,y)

determine
and are
uniquely determined by
a unique box
z=(p,y)\in\mathcal{R}_{dn,dm}^{+}
as in Figure
4
. Moreover,

(1)

x<p

\Leftrightarrow

z\in D
. In this case,
(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{l})(z)=(p-x-1,y_{p}-y)
. So,

\mathrm{rk}(x,y)>\mathrm{rk}(p,y_{p})\penalty\ \penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ m(\mathfrak{a}(z)+1)<n\mathfrak{l}(z)\penalty\ \penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \frac{\mathfrak{l}(z)}{\mathfrak{a}(z)+1}>\frac{m}{n}.

It follows that
\mathrm{I}=\#\{z\in D:\frac{\mathfrak{l}(z)}{\mathfrak{a}(z)+1}>\frac{m}{n}\}
.

(2)

x>p

\Leftrightarrow

z\in\mathcal{R}_{dn,dm}^{+}\setminus D
. In this case,
(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{l})(z)=(x-p,y-y_{p}-1)
. So,

\mathrm{rk}(x,y)\geq\mathrm{rk}(p,y_{p})\penalty\ \penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ m\mathfrak{a}(z)\geq n(\mathfrak{l}(z)+1)\penalty\ \penalty\ \Leftrightarrow\penalty\ \penalty\ \frac{\mathfrak{l}(z)+1}{\mathfrak{a}(z)}\leq\frac{m}{n}.

It follows that
\mathrm{II}=\#\{z\in\mathcal{R}_{dn,dm}^{+}\setminus D:\frac{\mathfrak{l}(z)+1}{\mathfrak{a}(z)}\leq\frac{m}{n}\}
.

\mathfrak{l}(z)=y_{p}-y

Figure 4.
The box
z=(p,y)\in\mathcal{R}_{dn,dm}
and the pair
(p,y_{p})
,
(x,y)
with
y_{x}<y\leq y_{x+1}
determine each other. The red paths correspond to part of the Dyck path. Left:
x<p
,
\Leftrightarrow

z\in D
; Right:
x>p
,
\Leftrightarrow

z\in\mathcal{R}_{dn,dm}\setminus D
.

Finally, by
[
25
, Cor.3.4]
, we have

\mathrm{II}=|\mathcal{R}_{dn,dm}^{+}\setminus D|+\#\{z\in D:\frac{\mathfrak{l}(z)+1}{\mathfrak{a}(z)}\leq\frac{m}{n}\}=\delta-|D|+\#\{z\in D:\frac{\mathfrak{l}(z)+1}{\mathfrak{a}(z)}\leq\frac{m}{n}\}.

Altogether,
|\zeta(D)|=\delta-\#\{z\in D:\frac{\mathfrak{l}(z)}{\mathfrak{a}(z)+1}\leq\frac{m}{n}<\frac{\mathfrak{l}(z)+1}{\mathfrak{a}(z)}\}=\delta-\mathrm{dinv}(D)=\mathrm{co}\text{-}\mathrm{dinv}(D)
.
Done.
∎
